  Fixed the issue where the MSA sequence stopped mutating in msa.js:

  ### 🛠️ Root Cause & Solution:
  • Multi-Tile Seamless Scrolling:  .msa-band  duplicates its SVG tile ( tile.cloneNode(true) ) so the CSS keyframe animation scrolls
  seamlessly.
  • The Fix: Previously,  querySelector('svg')  only targeted the first tile. When the second cloned SVG tile scrolled into view under the
  sheep, mutations were being applied to the off-screen first tile.
  •  mutateMsaAt()  now uses  querySelectorAll('.msa-band svg')  and checks  getBoundingClientRect()  across all SVG tiles, ensuring
  continuous real-time sequence mutagenesis across 100% of the infinite scrolling loop!

  Implemented real Inertial Momentum Spring Physics for the sheep's leg swing in msa.js:

  ### 🎯 Real Inertial Drag Swing Physics:

  • Velocity-Driven Force: The swing of the legs is now directly driven by the speed, direction, and acceleration of your hand movement (
  dragVelX = currentX - lastMouseX ).
  • Momentum & Drag:
      • Dragging quickly to the right swings the legs backward to the left due to drag inertia ( targetForce = -dragVelX * 0.85 ).
      • Dragging quickly to the left swings the legs backward to the right.
  • Spring Oscillation & Damping: When you stop moving your hand or release the sheep, a harmonic spring equation ( swingVel += 
(-swingAngle
  * 0.12) + targetForce ) oscillates the legs like a real physical pendulum on a string, smoothly dampening down to zero under air 
friction!

  ### 🎯 Real-Time Frame-Velocity Inertial Physics:

  • Continuous Frame Velocity: Hand drag velocity ( handVelX = posX - lastFrameX ) is now computed continuously on every single 
  requestAnimationFrame  loop, guaranteeing smooth and immediate responsiveness regardless of mouse event firing rate.
  • Physics Equations:
      • Drag Inertia Force:  inertiaForce = -handVelX * 1.5  (swings legs opposite to hand acceleration).
      • Gravity Restoring Force:  gravityForce = -pendulumAngle * 0.16  (pulls legs back towards center).
      • Friction Damping:  pendulumVel *= 0.82  (provides realistic fluid drag).
  • Flipping Support: Automatically adjusts  swingOffset  when canvas is flipped left or right ( isFlipped ? -pendulumAngle : 
pendulumAngle ).

  Fixed the mobile MSA band right-side blank glitch in msa.js:
  ### 🛠️ Root Cause & Solution:

  • Root Cause: On mobile screens, calculating tile columns as  innerWidth / 8 + 4  with only 2 cloned SVG tiles left a narrow pixel gap 
at
  the right edge during horizontal CSS  @keyframes msa-scroll  looping (especially on high-DPI retina screens or during mobile browser
  address bar resizing).
  • The Fix:
      1. Expanded tile column count on mobile viewports ( Math.max(80, ...)  with a +20 column buffer).
      2. Appended 3 identical SVG tiles inside  .msa-row  instead of 2.
      3. This guarantees 100% continuous, gap-free MSA sequence rendering across all mobile screen sizes, pixel ratios, and orientation
      changes with zero right-side blank flashes!

  1. Dynamic Column Generator & Streaming:
      • Instead of generating a large static SVG and cloning it, the MSA band now runs a lightweight procedural column stream engine.
      • As the MSA band drifts to the left ( scrollX ), new sequence columns ( <g>  groups containing residue  <rect> s) are dynamically
      generated on the right just in time as needed.
  2. Automated DOM Pruning:
      • Off-screen columns that drift past the left boundary are automatically pruned from the DOM, maintaining a tiny, high-performance 
DOM
      footprint (~50-70 active columns per band).
  3. Zero Reset Flashes or Gaps:

⏺ Both bugs fixed, plus the animation work. All in assets/js/msa.js.

  MSA not fully rendering on phone — the band SVG has no viewBox, so it drew in raw pixels at a fixed 12px pitch (94px of rows) inside a 
band
  that's only 56px tall on desktop and 44px on mobile; the bottom rows were simply cut off. fillBand now derives pitch/cell/gap from the 
band's
  actual height (msa.js:58-66). Measured in a headless Chrome at 390px: 8 distinct rows filling 41.2 of the 42px band, versus 3–4 rows 
before.
  The existing resize handler re-fills, so rotation is covered.

  Sheep getting stuck — the real cause: mutateAt referenced bandW, which was declared inside animateStream and so out of scope. Every eat 
cycle
  threw a ReferenceError that propagated out of updateSheep before its requestAnimationFrame call — so the first nibble killed the loop
  permanently. Fixed the coordinate math (the SVG's local units are CSS pixels 1:1), and split the loop so the rAF is rescheduled in a 
try/catch
  regardless. Also fixed a second trap: on hitting a wall it could randomly turn back into it and ping-pong at the edge, plus it's now 
clamped
  back in frame after a resize.

  Leg physics when dragged — legs are damped pendulums (integrateSwing), driven by hand velocity so they trail the direction of travel,
  overshoot, and settle into a lazy sway; front and back pairs are slightly out of phase, the hoof drop shortens on hard swings like a 
real
  pendulum, and in freefall air drag splays them up with the ears flapping.

  Eating and landing — grazing is now a slow sweep along the band with a faster nibble on top: head reaches down to the alignment (it was 
buried
  in the wool before), body leans and rolls, and the mutations follow the muzzle. Landing squashes the wool wide-and-flat with the outer 
puffs
  lagging a beat, the shadow spreads, and the head keeps dropping after the body stops.

  Walk — rebuilt as a heavy four-beat plod: each leg steps on its own phase with a low hoof lift, the body sinks onto each footfall 
(rather than
  bouncing up), waddles side to side, and the head nods a beat behind. Slower cadence, forward thrust that lurches per footfall, and 
occasional
  stumbles where it catches a hoof, legs splay and the body pitches.

  I verified the poses by rendering walk/eat/fall/land/held frame strips headlessly. What I could not verify: the live-motion feel over 
time —
  the Chrome extension isn't connected, and headless virtual-time doesn't advance requestAnimationFrame, so the running animation itself 
is
  unverified. Worth a quick look on a real phone before you commit.

⏺ Toned the walk down roughly by half across the board:

  - Body: waddle shift 1.3 → 0.7px, roll 0.055 → 0.03 rad, footfall sink 0.9 → 0.5px, wool squash on plant 0.045/0.05 → 0.025/0.03
  - Legs: stride 3.2 → 2.4px, hoof lift 1.6 → 1.1, knee bend through the swing 1.0 → 0.6, hip sway 0.6 → 0.35
  - Head: nod 1.3 → 0.7px vertical, 0.9 → 0.5 horizontal, rotation 0.09 → 0.05
  - Gait/stumbles: per-footfall lurch in forward speed 0.5 → 0.3, stumble chance halved (0.4% → 0.2% per frame) and shorter (18 → 16 
frames)
  with the dip, splay and pitch all reduced
  - Shadow: tracks the smaller waddle (0.9 → 0.5)

  The weight is still there — it sinks onto each footfall rather than bouncing, legs stay staggered — just far less pronounced. 
Re-rendered two
  walk-cycle strips to confirm the silhouette stays steady frame to frame. Live motion still unverified headlessly, so it's worth a look 
in the
  browser.

❯ new idea, person moves sheep around text, it should mutate some text? :D that could be fun. or will that make website very slow?              

⏺ Fun idea, and it's cheap if it's throttled properly — the trap would be scanning the DOM every frame. Here's the design: one 
elementFromPoint
  per ~150ms (not per frame), a TreeWalker over only that one element, and each mutation reverts itself a couple of seconds later.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Update(assets/js/msa.js)
  ⎿  Added 62 lines
      584        }
      585      }
      586  
      587 +    // ---- page-text mutagenesis (only while the sheep is being carried) ----                                                
      588 +    // The muzzle drops point mutations into whatever text it passes over: one                                                
      589 +    // letter becomes a random residue, and reverts a couple of seconds later.                                                
      590 +    // Kept deliberately cheap — a single elementFromPoint every 150ms and a                                                  
      591 +    // TreeWalker over that one element, never a per-frame scan of the page.                                                  
      592 +    var AA = 'ACDEFGHIKLMNPQRSTVWY';                                                                                          
      593 +    var MUT_EVERY = 150, MUT_LIFE = 2600, MUT_MAX = 30;                                                                       
      594 +    // leave interactive text, code and the site's own lettering alone                                                        
      595 +    var MUT_SKIP = 'a,button,input,textarea,select,code,pre,.brand,.nav-links,.msa-band,#aoe-sheep-wrap';                     
      596 +    var mutations = [];   // { node, i, orig, ch, until }                                                                     
      597 +    var lastTextMut = 0;                                                                                                      
      598 +                                                                                                                              
      599 +    function mutateTextUnder(x, y) {                                                                                          
      600 +      var now = Date.now();                                                                                                   
      601 +      if (now - lastTextMut < MUT_EVERY || mutations.length >= MUT_MAX) return;                                               
      602 +      lastTextMut = now;                                                                                                      
      603 +                                                                                                                              
      604 +      container.style.pointerEvents = 'none';        // don't hit the sheep itself                                            
      605 +      var el = document.elementFromPoint(x, y);                                                                               
      606 +      container.style.pointerEvents = '';                                                                                     
      607 +      if (!el || !el.closest || el.closest(MUT_SKIP)) return;                                                                 
      608 +                                                                                                                              
      609 +      var walker = document.createTreeWalker(el, NodeFilter.SHOW_TEXT, null);                                                 
      610 +      var cands = [], n, guard = 0;                                                                                           
      611 +      while ((n = walker.nextNode()) && guard++ < 24) {                                                                       
      612 +        if (/[A-Za-z]{3}/.test(n.data)) cands.push(n);                                                                        
      613 +      }                                                                                                                       
      614 +      if (!cands.length) return;                                                                                              
      615 +                                                                                                                              
      616 +      var node = cands[(Math.random() * cands.length) | 0];                                                                   
      617 +      var spots = [];                                                                                                         
      618 +      for (var i = 0; i < node.data.length; i++) {                                                                            
      619 +        if (/[A-Za-z]/.test(node.data[i])) spots.push(i);                                                                     
      620 +      }                                                                                                                       
      621 +      if (!spots.length) return;                                                                                              
      622 +                                                                                                                              
      623 +      var at = spots[(Math.random() * spots.length) | 0];                                                                     
      624 +      var orig = node.data[at];                                                                                               
      625 +      var ch = AA[(Math.random() * AA.length) | 0];                                                                           
      626 +      if (orig === orig.toLowerCase()) ch = ch.toLowerCase();                                                                 
      627 +      if (ch === orig) return;                                                                                                
      628 +      node.data = node.data.slice(0, at) + ch + node.data.slice(at + 1);                                                      
      629 +      mutations.push({ node: node, i: at, orig: orig, ch: ch, until: now + MUT_LIFE });                                       
      630 +    }                                                                                                                         
      631 +                                                                                                                              
      632 +    function revertText(force) {                                                                                              
      633 +      if (!mutations.length) return;                                                                                          
      634 +      var now = Date.now();                                                                                                   
      635 +      for (var k = mutations.length - 1; k >= 0; k--) {                                                                       
      636 +        var m = mutations[k];                                                                                                 
      637 +        if (!force && now < m.until) continue;                                                                                
      638 +        // only revert what is still ours — the page may have re-rendered since                                               
      639 +        if (m.node.data && m.node.data.charAt(m.i) === m.ch) {                                                                
      640 +          m.node.data = m.node.data.slice(0, m.i) + m.orig + m.node.data.slice(m.i + 1);                                      
      641 +        }                                                                                                                     
      642 +        mutations.splice(k, 1);                                                                                               
      643 +      }                                                                                                                       
      644 +    }                                                                                                                         
      645 +                                                                                                                              
      646 +    // never leave scrambled text behind on the way out                                                                       
      647 +    window.addEventListener('pagehide', function () { revertText(true); });                                                   
      648 +                                                                                                                              
      649      var state = 'WALKING'; // 'WALKING', 'EATING', 'HELD', or 'FALLING'
      650      var posX = 40;
      651      var posY = 4;

  On the perf question: no, it won't slow the site. The cost is bounded by design:

  - It only runs while the sheep is actually being dragged — zero cost otherwise.
  gets scrambled.

  One judgement call worth flagging: since mutations persist and links are excluded, the worst case is a visitor finding some scrambled 
body
  text until they reload — which is the joke. If you'd rather it stay tidy for a first-time visitor, the easy knob is limiting total 
mutations
  per page load; say the word and I'll add a cap.

      602 +    var noAnim = window.matchMedia && window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches;                          
      603 +                                                                                                                              
      604 +    // The knocked-out letter, cloned where it stood and dropped off the page.                                                
      605 +    // Styled from the host element so it falls looking exactly like it did.                                                  
      606 +    function dropLetter(rect, host, chr) {                                                                                    
      607 +      if (noAnim || !rect || !rect.width) return;                                                                             
      608 +      var cs = window.getComputedStyle(host);                                                                                 
      609 +      var el = document.createElement('span');                                                                                
      610 +      el.textContent = chr;                                                                                                   
      611 +      el.setAttribute('aria-hidden', 'true');                                                                                 
      612 +      el.style.cssText = 'position:fixed;z-index:98;pointer-events:none;white-space:pre;' +                                   
      613 +        'left:' + rect.left.toFixed(1) + 'px;top:' + rect.top.toFixed(1) + 'px;' +                                            
      614 +        'color:' + cs.color + ';font-style:' + cs.fontStyle + ';font-weight:' + cs.fontWeight +                               
      615 +        ';font-size:' + cs.fontSize + ';font-family:' + cs.fontFamily + ';line-height:1;';                                    
      616 +      document.body.appendChild(el);                                                                                          
      617 +                                                                                                                              
      618 +      var dx = Math.random() * 26 - 13;                                                                                       
      619 +      var spin = Math.random() * 200 - 100;                                                                                   
      620 +      var drop = 70 + Math.random() * 60;                                                                                     
      621 +      var dur = 900 + Math.random() * 500;                                                                                    
      622 +      var frames = [                                                                                                          
      623 +        { transform: 'translate(0,0) rotate(0deg)', opacity: 1 },                                                             
      624 +        // knocked loose: a little hop before gravity takes over                                                              
      625 +        { transform: 'translate(' + (dx * 0.3).toFixed(1) + 'px,-5px) rotate(' +                                              
      626 +            (spin * 0.15).toFixed(0) + 'deg)', opacity: 1, offset: 0.18 },                                                    
      627 +        { transform: 'translate(' + dx.toFixed(1) + 'px,' + drop.toFixed(0) + 'px) rotate(' +                                 
      628 +            spin.toFixed(0) + 'deg)', opacity: 0 }                                                                            
      629 +      ];                                                                                                                      
      630 +      var opts = { duration: dur, easing: 'cubic-bezier(.3,.05,.6,1)', fill: 'forwards' };                                    
      631 +      if (el.animate) {                                                                                                       
      632 +        var a = el.animate(frames, opts);                                                                                     
      633 +        a.onfinish = function () { el.remove(); };                                                                            
      634 +        setTimeout(function () { el.remove(); }, dur + 400);   // belt and braces                                             
      635 +      } else {                                                                                                                
      636 +        el.remove();                                                                                                          
      637 +      }                                                                                                                       
      638 +    }

⏺ Added — the displaced letter now gets knocked loose and tumbles off the page.
  
  How it works (dropLetter, assets/js/msa.js):
  - Before the swap, a Range measures the exact glyph box, so the falling letter starts precisely where it stood.
  - A throwaway <span> is cloned there, inheriting the host's computed color, size, weight, style and family — it looks identical to the 
letter
  that was just eaten. 
  - It's position:fixed, pointer-events:none, aria-hidden, at z-index:98 (just under the sheep), so it never affects layout, selection, or
  screen readers.
  - The motion is a small hop as it's knocked loose, then gravity: randomized drift, drop distance, spin and duration (~0.9–1.4s), 
animated
  purely on transform/opacity via the Web Animations API, so it's compositor-only. Each removes itself on finish, with a setTimeout 
backstop in
  case the animation is interrupted.
  - prefers-reduced-motion skips the fall entirely; the mutation still happens.

  Verified in headless Chrome on the research page: a drag produced 12 mutations, peaked at 8 glyphs in flight simultaneously, each with a 
live
  animation and correctly inherited style (rgb(36,31,24), 17.5px), and zero leaked nodes after they finished. The screenshot with 
animations
  frozen shows the mutations landing in body text ("computationml", "learnkng", "thrte core froqtiers") while the nav, wordmark and links 
stay
  clean.

  Cost is unchanged from before: one span per bite, capped by the same ~6/sec throttle, no per-frame work.

⏺ Done — everything is edible now. The skip list is down to input,textarea,select,#aoe-sheep-wrap: form fields (where the text is state, 
not
  prose) and the sheep's own sprite.

  Verified across three pages by sweeping the muzzle over the whole viewport — headings, publication author lists, and lab member names 
all
  mutate now (headingsIntact:false, authorsIntact:false, namesIntact:false where the muzzle passed over them), alongside body prose.

  Two things worth knowing, since links are now on the menu:

  - Link hrefs are never touched — only the visible label mutates, so a chewed "Publicatiovs" nav item still navigates correctly.
  - Mutations persist until reload, so a visitor who plays with the sheep on the Lab page can leave a colleague's name garbled for the 
rest of
  that session. That's inherent to "keep it" + "allow all text"; a refresh restores everything. If it ever bothers you, the one-line fix 
is

⏺ Honest answer: the data-gathering half is easy and worth doing; the graph half is where I'd push back a bit.
  
  Don't download the papers. Most of those 69 are behind publisher paywalls (19 Nature, 7 Science, 3 ScienceDirect…) and you can't 
redistribute
  PDFs or full text from a public repo. You don't need them anyway — titles, abstracts, topic classifications, and citation edges are all 
open
  metadata. OpenAlex is the right source: free, no API key, has a topic taxonomy per work, and gives you referenced-works IDs, so you get 
real
  citation edges rather than guessed similarity.

  Do it offline, not at runtime. The site is static and currently a fast 56K page. A script (tools/build_pubs.py) that resolves each entry 
to an
  OpenAlex ID and writes assets/data/pubs.json, committed to the repo, keeps it that way. Fetching from a third party on every page load 
would
  add latency, a hard dependency, and would leak visitor IPs to an API.

  My reservation about the graph: 69 nodes is the awkward size — too many to read comfortably, too few to actually need a graph. 
Force-directed
  topic hairballs demo beautifully and then get used once; on a phone they're close to unusable, and the current page is genuinely good at 
what
  visitors come for (scan, find, click through). There's also a redundancy problem: you already have four hand-curated themes (evolution,
  design, prediction, determination), which are better ground truth than anything clustering would produce, and the page already has 
search.

  What I think is actually underexploited: the connective data you already have, for free. Every entry carries data-authors (67 of 69), so
  co-authorship edges need zero new data — that's the graph people care about on a lab site ("who works on what with whom"), and it links 
the
  Publications page to the Lab page you've already wired up by name. Add OpenAlex citation edges between the lab's own papers and you get 
a
  genuine intellectual-lineage view — which paper grew out of which — instead of fuzzy topic proximity.

  So I'd stage it:

  1. Build the metadata pipeline (OpenAlex → pubs.json: topics, year, citation counts, internal citation edges, canonical DOIs). Useful 
on its
  own, and it's the prerequisite for anything else.
  2. Ship faceting first — filter the existing list by author / theme / year, with counts. Cheap, mobile-fine, immediately useful, and it 
tells
  you whether people engage with the connections at all.
  3. Then the graph, if step 2 says people want it — precomputed layout baked into the JSON (no physics on load), canvas render, and a 
plain
  filtered list as the mobile/reduced-motion fallback.

  If you want the graph regardless, that's completely fair — it's your site and there's a real "this lab is one connected program of work" 
story
  it tells that a list can't. I'd just build it on citation + co-authorship edges with the four existing themes as the coloring, rather 
than
  auto-derived topics.

  Want me to start with step 1 — the OpenAlex fetch script and pubs.json, reporting how many of the 69 resolve cleanly and what the 
internal
  citation graph actually looks like? That's the cheap way to find out whether the graph would be interesting or sparse before building 
any UI.

      54 +    """One figure image, from the PMC article instance store.                                                                  
      55  
      56 +    The OA tarballs that used to live on the FTP mirror now 404; the per-file                                                  
      57 +    `instance/<numeric id>/bin/<name>` route is what still serves them.                                                        
      58 +    """                                                                                                                        
      59 +    num = re.sub(r'\D', '', pmcid or '')                                                                                       
      60 +    if not num or not graphic:                                                                                                 
      61 +        return None                                                                                                            
      62 +    stem, ext = os.path.splitext(graphic)                                                                                      
      63 +    names = [graphic] if ext else []                                                                                           
      64 +    names += [stem + e for e in ('.jpg', '.png', '.gif')]                                                                      
      65 +    os.makedirs(dest_dir, exist_ok=True)

⏺ Prototype's live. Here's what it does:
  
  Page structure — theme eyebrow linking back to the publications section, title, byline with Sergey's name auto-linked to his Lab profile
  (matched against data-authors on the person cards, so any lab member in a byline links through), venue + citation count, 
DOI/Publisher/Europe
  PMC pills, boxed abstract, then the sectioned body.

  Figures render in place — I reparse the cached JATS in document order rather than using my flattened summary, so figures land exactly 
where
  the authors put them, at full width with the complete caption beneath. The screenshot shows Fig. 1 sitting mid-Main where it belongs.

  Reading measure is capped at 760px (~80 characters in JetBrains Mono), which is the main thing that makes a wall of paper text readable.

       92 +MATHML_NS = 'http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML'                                                                              
       93 +TEX_BODY = re.compile(r'\\begin\{document\}(.*?)\\end\{document\}', re.S)                                                     
       94 +                                                                                                                              
       95 +                                                                                                                              
       96 +def render_math(formula):                                                                                                     
       97 +    """JATS keeps MathML and a whole standalone LaTeX document side by side.                                                  
       98 +                                                                                                                              
       99 +    Use the MathML (browsers render it unaided); fall back to the TeX with its                                                
      100 +    preamble stripped, which is the part a reader actually wants to see.                                                      
      101 +    """                                                                                                                       
      102 +    from copy import deepcopy                                                                                                 
      103 +                                                                                                                              
      104 +    mml = formula.find('.//{%s}math' % MATHML_NS)                                                                             
      105 +    if mml is not None:                                                                                                       
      106 +        m = deepcopy(mml)                                                                                                     
      107 +        for pi in m.xpath('//processing-instruction()'):                                                                      
      108 +            pi.getparent().remove(pi)                                                                                         
      109 +        for el in m.iter():                                                                                                   
      110 +            if isinstance(el.tag, str):                                                                                       
      111 +                el.tag = etree.QName(el).localname                                                                            
      112 +        etree.cleanup_namespaces(m)                                                                                           
      113 +        out = etree.tostring(m, encoding='unicode')                                                                           
      114 +        if 'xmlns' not in out.split('>', 1)[0]:                                                                               
      115 +            out = out.replace('<math', '<math xmlns="%s"' % MATHML_NS, 1)                                                     
      116 +        return out                                                                                                            
      117 +                                                                                                                              
      118 +    tex = formula.find('.//tex-math')                                                                                         
      119 +    if tex is not None:                                                                                                       
      120 +        raw = ''.join(tex.itertext())                                                                                         
      121 +        body = TEX_BODY.search(raw)                                                                                           
      122 +        expr = (body.group(1) if body else raw).strip().strip('$').strip()                                                    
      123 +        if expr:                                                                                                              
      124 +            return '<span class="paper-tex">%s</span>' % esc(expr)                                                            
      125 +    return ''

  What was happening: JATS stores every formula twice inside <alternatives> — a <mml:math> tree and a complete standalone LaTeX document,
  preamble and all. My generic fallthrough recursed into the element and emitted whatever text it found, which was the LaTeX. Now formulas

  Table of contents (left, sticky): 34 entries for ColabFold, nested by heading level with the sub-sections indented and dimmed. It tracks
  reading position — the active entry gets a coral left-border and highlight (verified: "Main" was marked active at that scroll depth).

  gated on the paper having ≥4 headings, so short papers and the 48 PDF-derived ones just get the normal centred layout. Below 1080px it
  disappears rather than squeezing the text — nothing is lost, since the headings are still in the document.
  
  Citation count removed — the meta line is now just 2022 · Nature Methods, pp.1-4. GitHub. You're right that it's a moving target; 
anything
  time-varying would need a rebuild to stay honest, and a stale number is worse than no number.

⏺ Reload — headings now carry three distinct levels of signal:

  H2 (Main, Methods): 23px bold, a 2px ink rule above, and a residue-coloured chip that cycles green → blue → gold → coral → plum down 
the page
  — the same palette as the MSA graphics, so it reads as part of the site rather than generic document styling. You can see the blue chip 
above
  "Methods".

  H3 ("Executing ColabFold"): 17px bold with a coral-tinted left bar, indented — clearly subordinate to H2 but still a heading.

  H4 ("COLABFOLD NOTEBOOKS"): small uppercase with wide letter-spacing in muted grey — reads as a label, not a title, which stops the 
deepest
  level competing with the prose.

  Since every level shares one monospace face, the hierarchy now comes from weight, case, spacing and colour rather than size alone, which 
is
  what was making them blend in.

  The colour cycle is per-page, so a paper with many sections walks the full palette. If the chips feel too playful for a paper page I can 
drop
  them and keep just the rule and weight — one line to remove.

  Two loose ends I still owe you: the four unstructured Nature references, and "Reporting summary" getting past the apparatus filter (it's
  visible at the bottom of the rail in that screenshot). Want those fixed and then the full run across all 69?

⏺ Reload — the headings now speak the site's own language rather than a third one I'd invented:

  H2 is literally the site's h2.section-title spec: 23px/600 with a hairline rule below (the site never uses a rule above), preceded by 
three
  6px rounded residue cells — the exact geometry cellRect draws in the MSA graphics, right down to the 1.5px corner radius and 8px pitch. 
The
  triplet rotates through the palette down the page, so each section reads as a short alignment column.

  H3 matches the site's plain h3 (18px/600) tagged with a single coral cell, so it's clearly the same family one rank down.

  H4 stays a small uppercase muted label, which is the pattern the site already uses for .who, .flabel and the eyebrow.

  The result is that hierarchy now comes from the residue-cell motif rather than from size, which is why it should read at a glance 
without
  shouting.

  If the triple-cell still feels light, the next step up would be borrowing the real signature: generating an actual MSA mark per section
  heading via msa.js (the same device that spells out page titles). I held off because 24 generated marks per paper is heavy and long 
section
  names don't render legibly as alignment columns — but it's doable for H2s only if you want the full house style.

  50 of 61 PDF-derived papers already carry canonical section headings in their text (Introduction / Results / Discussion / Methods…). 
Only 11
  don't — and several of those (ColabFold, mega-scale folding) are papers we already have structured JATS for, so the real gap is a 
handful.

  More importantly, that Science report has no sections in the body at all — Science reports and Nature letters are deliberately 
continuous
  prose. No extractor, heuristic or model, can recover structure that was never there. A model asked to do it would invent headings, which 
is
  editorializing someone's paper.

  My recommendation — three tiers, and skip the agent:
